She’ll say, “That’s how work is,” or “You’re reading too much into it.”\n\nBut it’s hard, because for about a year now these things have lined up in a way that feels too exact. Like… not one meeting, not one odd message, but the timing, the wording, the little shifts. It reminds me of when I was working around faculty calendars—people would say a change was nothing, and then later you’d find out there was a reason it had been moved.\n\nSo I don’t know if she’s fully aware of how I’m interpreting it, or if she’s pretending not to be. But I do think there’s something in it, something private, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself looking for hidden meanings or patterns in things other people do or say, beyond just your wife's work schedule and messages?\n\nPatient: Not really beyond that, no… or at least, I don’t think so. I mean, I notice patterns sometimes—anyone who’s spent time around university schedules does, because meetings and wording and timing, it all has a kind of structure if you’re paying attention.\n\nBut the thing with my wife is different. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and maybe other people would leave it there. But for the past year, they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental to me. It feels like there’s something private in it, like a signal meant to tell me something she won’t just say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself looking for hidden meanings in things that other people say or do, outside of your wife's work schedule and messages?\n\nPatient: Outside of that? I don’t think I do, not often. I mean, sometimes I notice how people phrase things, but that’s probably just from years of being around academic work, where a small change in wording can mean something completely different.\n\nBut with my wife’s schedule and messages, it feels different. She keeps saying the late meetings and small changes are just normal office things, and maybe someone else would accept that. It’s just that over the past year they’ve lined up too neatly, too precisely. It feels like there’s some private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever stopped believing that your wife's work schedule and messages contain hidden signals, even if only for a short period?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a moment, yes… like if I’m busy with classes or grading, I can tell myself it’s just office scheduling and I should leave it alone. You know, when you’re buried in student papers, everything else kind of gets pushed to the side.\n\nBut then another late meeting shows up, or the wording in one of her messages shifts just a little, and it all comes back. It’s been about a year of that. She says it’s normal work stuff, and I try to hear that, but it lines up too precisely for me. It feels like there’s something private in it, like she’s signaling something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you completely stopped believing in the hidden signals in your wife's work schedule and messages?\n\nPatient: Completely stopped? No… I don’t think completely. I mean, there are times when I’m distracted—teaching, grading, all the little things that pile up, and the department has its own strange rhythms too, so schedules can look odd for perfectly boring reasons.\n\nBut even then, it’s more like I push it aside, not that I stop believing it. Because when I look back at the late meetings and the little wording changes in her work messages, over this past year they’ve just lined up too precisely. She says it’s normal office stuff, and I know that’s what she says, but it still feels like there’s something private in it—like a signal, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a few weeks where you didn't think about the hidden signals in your wife's work schedule and messages?\n\nPatient: More than a few weeks? No, I don’t think so. Maybe I’d go a few days where I’m caught up with teaching or grading, or some departmental thing—there’s always some form to fill out or student issue—and I’m not actively thinking about it.\n\nBut it hasn’t really gone away for weeks at a time. I know my wife says the late meetings and those little changes in her work messages are normal office stuff. I try to take that in. It’s just… for the past year, they’ve lined up too neatly to feel accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a month where you didn't think about the hidden signals in your wife's work schedule and messages?\n\nPatient: More than a month? No, not really. It’s been going on about a year now, since my wife started working outside the home again.\n\nI mean, at first I thought maybe it was just the adjustment, you know, new routines, new people, different hours—everyone acts like schedules are just schedules, but when a household changes, you notice things. I remember when I started teaching, even my own evenings got strange for a while. But with her, since she went back to work, that’s when I started noticing the messages and the timing. So no, I can’t say there’s been a whole month where it just wasn’t on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you completely stopped thinking about the hidden signals in your wife's work schedule and messages for any reason?\n\nPatient: Completely stopped thinking about it? No… I don’t think so. I mean, I can be busy with teaching work, or I’ll get pulled into some ordinary thing like emails piling up—funny how one message can change the whole tone of a day, actually—but it doesn’t really disappear.\n\nI know my wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff. And maybe people at offices do have strange schedules, I’m not saying they don’t. But over this past year, they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t just say directly. So no, I don’t think there’s been a time when I completely stopped.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your wife's work schedule and messages contain hidden signals ever completely gone away?\n\nPatient: No, I can’t say it’s ever completely gone away. I’ll have moments where I’m distracted—teaching, grading, some student asking about an assignment from three weeks ago, that kind of thing—but then something in her schedule or a message will stand out again.\n\nI know she says it’s just normal office things, late meetings and small changes in wording or timing. And offices do have their own rhythms, I suppose. But for the past year it’s just lined up too neatly for me to believe it’s all accidental. It feels like there’s something private in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it straight out.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your wife's work schedule and messages contain hidden signals ever changed over the past year?\n\nPatient: Changed? Not really in the big sense. I mean, some days I’m more tired and I don’t follow every little thing as closely—like when I’ve got papers to grade, and those can pile up in a ridiculous way, you know, students all using the same excuses—but the basic feeling hasn’t changed much.\n\nMy wife still says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things. Maybe that’s what it looks like from the outside. But over this past year, they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental to me. It’s like there’s a private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your wife's work schedule and messages contain hidden signals ever weakened or become less certain at any point?\n\nPatient: Weakened… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. There are times I’m not focused on it as much, like if I’m dealing with classes or some student problem—last week someone emailed me three times about the same missing citation, and honestly that kind of thing can take over your head for a while.\n\nBut less certain? Not really. I know my wife says the late meetings and those little changes in her work messages are just ordinary office things, and I’m sure offices do have plenty of that. But over the past year they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel it’s accidental. It still seems like there’s a private signal in there, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your wife's work schedule and messages contain hidden signals ever completely disappeared, even if only for a brief moment?\n\nPatient: Completely disappeared? No, I don’t think so. I mean, maybe if I’m busy with teaching or marking papers, I’m not sitting there thinking about it every second—grading can swallow a whole evening if the students all make the same mistake, and then you start wondering if anyone listened in the first place—but that’s different.\n\nIt’s still there underneath. I know my wife says the late meetings and the small changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and maybe that’s how she explains it. But for the past year they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental to me. It feels like there’s a private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't think about the hidden signals in your wife's work schedule and messages for at least a couple of months straight?\n\nPatient: A couple of months straight? No, I don’t think so. It’s been sort of there since my wife started working outside the home again, which was about a year ago now.\n\nI mean, there are stretches where I’m distracted—work gets busy, students need things, and the semester has its own way of eating up your attention—but not months where it was gone. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a point where you were absolutely certain that your wife's work schedule and messages did not contain any hidden signals?\n\nPatient: Absolutely certain there were no signals? No, I can’t say that. I mean, I’ve tried to tell myself maybe it’s just her office being disorganized—people changing meetings, sending oddly worded messages, all that. Universities are like that too, honestly. One committee email can have three different meanings depending on who sent it, and then everyone pretends it was clear.\n\nBut with my wife’s schedule and the little changes in her messages… over this past year, they’ve lined up too precisely. So no, I don’t remember a point where I was sure there was nothing there. It still feels like some kind of private signal, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t just say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that other people or events were sending you hidden messages or signals, outside of your wife's work?\n\nPatient: Other people or events? No, not really in the same way. I mean, people say things all the time that can sound pointed if you’re already tired—students especially, they’ll send an email at midnight with some “just checking” phrase and you think, well, what does that mean? But that’s just… academic life, I suppose.\n\nThe thing I keep coming back to is my wife’s work. The late meetings, the small changes in her messages, the way they line up over the past year—it feels too precise to be accidental. Like there’s something private in it, some signal meant for me, something she won’t just say plainly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that anyone or anything else besides your wife's work was sending you hidden signals?\n\nPatient: No, not anyone else, not really. I mean, sometimes you can read too much into things—like at the university, if someone changes the wording on a memo or a student sends a strange email, you wonder for a second what they’re getting at. But that’s not the same.\n\nIt’s my wife’s work schedule and her messages that feel… specific. She says the late meetings and little changes are just normal office stuff, and maybe offices are like that, I know. But over the past year they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental. It feels like there’s some private signal in them meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a period, no matter how short, when you didn't believe your wife's work schedule and messages contained hidden signals?\n\nPatient: A period when I didn’t believe it? I don’t think so, no. I mean, maybe there are individual moments where I’m distracted—grading papers or trying to fix the printer in the department office, which somehow is always broken right before I need it—but when I come back to it, it’s still there.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know offices can be messy like that. But for the past year they’ve lined up too neatly for me to feel like it’s accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in them, something meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
